# S05 — Seeds: predictions, wishes, and experiments that could settle disagreements

Research date: **2026-10-28**. Theme: the seed hunter. **I — Recommendation:** investigate ordinary people's small software before building another agent platform. The leading seeds are a family-app demand experiment, an executable retirement kit for personal apps, and a blind product-insight rediscovery experiment. These are conditional openings. This search did **not** establish an empty, well-specified product category with the strength of the original purpose-built-kernel observation.

**Q** = supplied caption quotation or speaker claim; **F** = checked source/repository fact; **I** = interpretation, bounded search conclusion, proposed artifact, or ranking; **E** = effort, acceptance threshold, or subjective score. Quotes establish what was said; numerical forecasts and demos are not independent measurements. Caption times are minute buckets. Brackets and ellipses omit words without changing their order. The source brief's prohibited superlative is omitted as `[…]` where it occurs inside copied source quotations.

## Scope, coverage, and how to use this report

**F — Reading:** read in full the per-video digests, recurring themes/disagreements, prior-art triage and top-five sections in E01–E11, together with the five Connect ledgers' corresponding sections. Read every row typed Prediction, Wish/gap, Open question, or Disagreement, including mixed types and Connect's `W` and `Pr` shorthand. Read the additional roadmap rows and the supplemental modal-language rows in the board below. Returned to the important caption passages, particularly videos 11–16, 18, 22, 30–31, 34, 52, 79–80 and 85. Consulted [the brief](/Users/noah/code/sofort/arch/research/corpus/BRIEF-synthesis.md), [research summary §§11–16](/Users/noah/code/sofort/arch/research/RESEARCH-SUMMARY.md), [stack summary](/Users/noah/code/sofort/arch/research/stack/SUMMARY.md), and [Connect summary](/Users/noah/code/sofort/arch/research/connect26/SUMMARY.md).

**F — Inventory correction:** the repository supplies **96 unique transcript files**, comprising 83 in `corpus/transcripts` and 13 in `connect26/transcripts`. The [playlist manifest](/Users/noah/code/sofort/arch/research/corpus/README.md) includes 88 entries, of which #02, #06 and #07 duplicate Connect talks; #27 is rate-limited/missing and #81 has no captions. Thus the stated **98-video inventory** is accounted for, but two videos cannot contribute textual evidence. No claim is made to have recovered their predictions. The Next Token Ep 07 kernel sentence in the task is a motivating example outside this board, not an extra independent vote for any seed here.

**F — Board coverage:** 1,392 requested-type rows, 286 additional roadmap rows, and 239 supplemental rows = **1,917 source-indexed rows**, without duplicating a row carrying multiple types. Some selected rows are questions, normative wishes, jokes, historical statements, or claims that something already exists; keeping them prevents an optimistic novelty filter. A supplemental row is a recall aid, not automatically a prediction. Every row carries a speaker, time, source link, quote and conditional build implication. This is exhaustive over the specified ledger types and the stated supplemental scan, rather than a claim that every sentence of all 96 transcripts was independently re-extracted.

**I — Negative evidence:** “Open” below means the exact narrow artifact was not located in this bounded search. It does not mean nobody has built it. Product claims are documentation availability, not hands-on validation. “Being built” requires a public implementation or active work; “Roadmap” is a stated intention. Existing projects retain their credit. The board preserves extraction ideas for traceability; only P01–P20 are recommendations considered in this synthesis.

## Ranking method and the useful convergence

**I — Selection:** rank by (a) specificity/buildability, (b) practitioner credibility and independent corroboration, (c) field emptiness after functional searches, then the builder's six criteria. A narrow reproducible experiment can beat a speculative product even with a low novelty score. The final table exposes these dimensions separately; it is an ordered judgment, not a market-size calculation. No project earns more than 3/5 for field emptiness. A strong quote alone cannot overcome direct prior art.

**I — Independence:** the counts below are conservative named anchor counts, not corpus-wide prevalence estimates. Repeated appearances by one person count once. A host interviewing different guests does not turn one channel into several. Connect is one vendor event/channel; Cloudflare employees' forecasts, customer deployments, sponsor reads and investors' endorsements are not independent corroboration of demand. Theo and Ben debating each other provide a testable disagreement, not two confirmations of the same forecast. The Karpathy compilation is secondary evidence, not an independent channel for each clip.

## Current field map: what searches actually changed

**F — Search date:** 2026-10-28. Functional and named searches used public GitHub repositories/READMEs, npm registry search/metadata, product documentation/changelogs, research papers, blogs, HN's 2026 story index and targeted X searches. npm's broad OR matching returned much irrelevant material; counts are not vacancy evidence. HN exposed Powermove and effective-expressiveness research. Targeted X queries for malleable customization, disposable software and product rediscovery returned no useful indexed primary result; coverage there is incomplete. These checks were per cluster and shortlisted artifact, not a claim that 1,917 separate markets received equal research.

**I — Prior-art reversals:** the strongest negative result is the game catchup: a source quote that sounds perfectly seed-shaped already has remarkably close implementations. The same happened to malleable video editing and service-fake distribution. The kernel analogy is useful as a search method, not a license to call each wish unbuilt.

## Project cards

All starts, demos and kill thresholds below are **I/E**, not work already performed. The six scores, always in order, are **cool / Cloudflare–Vercel–TypeScript adjacency / real gap / value / survives better models / not GrowOS**. A high survival score means the underlying need or experiment remains meaningful, not that a durable company follows. “Platform ownership” explains where an independent contribution could fit; it is not a moat claim.

### P01 — Tailor once or keep editing? A family-app field experiment

**Q — Sources:** Sunil, [V12 51:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gPBw5Cr9VJo&t=3060s), “How do you actually make lives better?” and 53:00, “for your family for your friends.” Ben/Modem, [V80 1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HDdbU10r7Js&t=5580s), “I spent it like an hour ... appealing to my family. And I'm good.” Geoff, 1:32, “society at large does not want an assistant.”

**I — One sentence:** test whether a one-time tailored family tool produces more lasting benefit than either an ordinary shared tool or an app that invites continuous AI editing. **Size/E:** Big as a three-to-four-week field study; Small for a weekend prototype. **I — Beneficiaries:** non-programmer households, community organizers and builders deciding what kind of personal software to offer.

**E — 30-second demo:** show the same weekly family coordination task in three conditions: an existing shared tool, a tailored static app, and an editable version; then show actual completed tasks, return use, editing requests and maintenance time after several weeks. Do not equate more edits with more value.

**F/I — Prior art:** [Potluck](https://www.inkandswitch.com/potluck/), [Manyshape](https://github.com/Them-labs/manyshape), and the [malleable-software research](https://www.inkandswitch.com/essay/malleable-software/) supply mechanisms and human studies. The residual is a bounded non-programmer longitudinal comparison, not a new app generator. **Status:** Open experiment; occupied technology.

**I — Platform ownership:** providers can ship editors; they cannot infer a particular household's desired routine or establish independent cross-product demand. Use existing hosting and generation. **Start:** recruit six to twelve consenting households with one recurring, currently awkward task; interview them before proposing an app; counterbalance order and preserve an existing-tool baseline. Record task success and voluntary edit demand without reminding people to edit. **Kill:** if families prefer their ordinary tool, or maintenance exceeds saved effort, stop the software idea and publish that result. If tailoring works but ongoing edits do not, ship a one-time service/template. Small samples reveal mechanisms, not population-level demand.

**E — Scores:** **4 / 4 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 5**. **Buildability/credibility/emptiness:** **5 / 3 / 3**; strong practitioner convergence, weak evidence about ordinary users, direct disagreement concentrated in one podcast.

### P02 — A personal app that can retire without losing its point

**Q — Sources:** Max Kanat-Alexander, [V79 09:00–12:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bU5gDVyu_28&t=540s), “Write a party planner software just for that one party. Throw it away”; “You can make software more disposable”; “I thought this was going to be disposable and now it needs to be durable.” His adjoining conditions are limited data, no production dependents and a durable fallback.

**I — One sentence:** build a small TypeScript retirement contract that exports chosen records, produces a useful static fallback, rehearses restoration and flags dependencies that make retirement inappropriate. **Size/E:** Big, two weeks for one supported app shape; Small for a reference template. **Beneficiaries:** people making temporary meetup/party/club tools and maintainers who inherit them.

**E — Demo:** close a party-planning app; its useful guest-approved records and final plan remain readable without the original service, restoration is exercised, and timers/access are retired. A second fixture with an inbound client fails the “disposable” check and prompts promotion to maintained software. Use synthetic demonstration data.

**F/I — Prior art:** [Manyshape](https://github.com/Them-labs/manyshape) owns disposable personal surfaces and regeneration; [Vibe's retirement notice](https://github.com/MicrosoftDocs/powerapps-docs/blob/main/powerapps-docs/vibe/migrate-apps.md) and [operating advice](https://sam-solutions.us/insights/disposable-software-business-apps/) show lifecycle/export is existing territory. The narrow Open hypothesis is the executable combination for one app shape. This extends the earlier exit-drill candidate; it does not reopen generic migration, sandboxing or generated-app hosting.

**I — Platform ownership:** platforms own retention timers and exports, while the owner chooses what records matter and what fallback counts as success. A cross-provider reference template can exercise that policy. **Start:** implement one party app with a SQLite-backed Worker or static app plus export, explicit lifetime/data/dependent/fallback fields and a rehearsal command. Do not attempt universal dependency discovery. **Kill:** if existing export plus a static page meets every fixture with little work, publish a recipe rather than a library; if real apps cannot enumerate consumers, label the result a declaration requiring owner review, not proof of disposability.

**E — Scores:** **4 / 5 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 5**. **Buildability/credibility/emptiness:** **4 / 4 / 3**; specific experienced practitioner, no independent confirmation of demand for this package.

### P03 — Can an agent rediscover the product insight you hid?

**Q — Source:** Theo, [V14 1:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H0H_zhKnu3U&t=4440s), “I'm going to create an isolated environment to see how long until I can get the agents to come up with something similar or better.” The debate is whether a good sidebar/product choice can be verified or induced, rather than merely implemented from instructions.

**I — One sentence:** publish a small blind rediscovery protocol using original user pain and pre-solution repositories, with the builder's later insight withheld and useful alternative solutions accepted. **Size/E:** Tiny/Small weekend protocol and three cases; weeks only if a real corpus emerges. **Beneficiaries:** product builders deciding what judgment remains human and evaluators who need tasks beyond implementing a supplied specification.

**E — Demo:** a split view reveals a hidden human solution only after three runs: no hints, preference history, and one discriminating hint; show blind user-task outcomes and cost, not whether the agent copied the secret design.

**F/I — Prior art:** [Kombai](https://kombai.com/benchmark/), [open-design-system-bench](https://github.com/christophhdesign/open-design-system-bench), [Zero2Repo](https://zero2repo.ai/), and [FIRE-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02905) already implement adjacent creativity/hidden-answer/rediscovery evaluations. Conditional Open scope is the **unspecified product choice with externally judged utility**, not generic agent creativity or hidden tests.

**I — Platform ownership:** labs can evaluate coding but real pre-insight artifacts and independent users are distributed among builders. A small open protocol can be contributed to an existing benchmark. **Start:** obtain three consented examples with original pain notes and a repository state before the insight; remove later git history, docs and metadata leaks; pre-register task acceptance and equal tool/budget conditions. Have judges unaware of condition and solution authorship; report disagreement. **Kill:** if user outcomes cannot distinguish choices reproducibly, call it a case study, not a leaderboard. If models rediscover or improve on the insight cheaply, publish that falsification and retire the human-taste claim.

**E — Scores:** **5 / 5 / 3 / 4 / 4 / 5**. **Buildability/credibility/emptiness:** **5 / 3 / 3**; very concrete wish, direct disagreement from one show, related independent pain without proof of a permanent capability limit.

### P04 — An author's current-view and applicability editor

**Q — Source:** Gergely Orosz, [V18 1:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Prl858_AovM&t=6480s), “my opinions change all the time. My views change.” His bot repeats old writing; the adjacent tech-lead-manager example changes applicability, not simply truth value.

**I — One sentence:** add an author-facing view that marks statements current, historical, context-dependent or awaiting confirmation, and shows the effect on an answer. **Size/E:** Small prototype, Big only with an author partner. **Beneficiaries:** authors, readers and maintainers of expert avatars. **Demo/E:** ask the same management question for two eras/team conditions; preserve the historical quote while the owner approves a present qualification.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Delphi](https://help.delphi.co/articles/16041852-add-knowledge-train-mind) already has owner corrections/context and [Graphiti](https://github.com/getzep/graphiti) temporal storage; [supersession research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15710) explicitly distinguishes suggested change from confirmed replacement. **Being built/Shipped** broadly; narrow applicability-review UI **Open hypothesis**. **Platform ownership:** Delphi could readily own this. Prefer an upstream UI/fixture contribution or portable authoring format. **Start:** annotate twenty real statements with their author; prohibit model-only supersession. **Kill:** if per-source context and owner corrections express all cases clearly, stop at documentation examples. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 2 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 3 / 2**.

### P05 — Persona 3 catchup, as a contribution to an existing project

**Q — Source:** Theo, [V16 1:39–1:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4zMgyhXeGig&t=5940s), “I didn't remember like where I was ... what my like strategy with my party was.” He wants story, party, combat and next objective after a long break; his workaround already collects state through game control.

**I — One sentence:** contribute a read-only Persona 3 state adapter and progression-bounded recap fixtures to a save-aware assistant. **Size/E:** Small, conditional on documented format; parsing may take weeks. **Beneficiaries:** returning players. **Demo/E:** load an abandoned save and get a party/strategy recap with later plot information absent from retrieved material.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Savecraft](https://savecraft.gg/) is directly adjacent; [GameBuddy](https://github.com/Priyansh-Shukla/Gamebuddy) explicitly has spoiler-safe session recall architecture, although real-game parser discovery remains unfinished. Broad concept **Shipped/Being built**, not an empty category. **Platform ownership:** platforms or publishers could do this; open community adapters cover long-tail games. **Start:** check current Persona 3 support, format/license constraints and maintainer interest; build two synthetic progression fixtures before writing a parser. **Kill:** if current support works, just document it; if no reliable progression boundary can be derived, offer player notes instead of promising spoiler guarantees. **E — Scores:** **5 / 3 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 2 / 1**.

### P06 — One provider-owned semantic fake, with conformance cases

**Q — Source:** Max, [V79 14:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bU5gDVyu_28&t=840s), “everybody would provide in-memory fake versions of their services.” This is a wish for provider responsibility, not just agent-generated mocks.

**I — One sentence:** contribute a fake for one real stateful endpoint, sharing semantic examples with a live-service conformance run. **Size/E:** Tiny upstream package/PR or Small weekend spike. **Beneficiaries:** SDK users and the provider's support team. **Demo/E:** replay create/retry/read/delete cases against fake and test account; expose a matching schema but mismatching retry behavior.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Microcks Hub](https://microcks.io/documentation/guides/integration/microcks-hub/) already distributes producer packages and [LocalStack](https://www.localstack.cloud/) implements broad emulation. **Shipped** mechanisms; gap depends on an actual missing endpoint. **Platform ownership:** the provider is the desired owner. An independent builder contributes, rather than starts a competing registry. **Start:** ask one OSS-friendly provider to identify a support-heavy operation and review semantics. **Kill:** no provider review or no difference from existing mocks means no package. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 2 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 4 / 2**.

### P07 — Private on-phone classifier parity, on the real task

**Q — Sources:** Ree, [V11 15:00–18:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bs3xRsDItiI&t=900s), “data needs to stay on the users's machine”; Sunil wants something he can “run on my phone.” The discussed classifier quality is an observation, not a controlled benchmark.

**I — One sentence:** test current local classifiers on an actual private attention/tool-selection workload, measuring abstention, quality, latency and phone energy. **Size/E:** Small experiment; device matrix later. **Beneficiaries:** people whose messages/tool metadata cannot leave their devices. **Demo/E:** the phone stays offline, classifies synthetic sensitive messages, and abstains on an unfamiliar tool; a local result sheet compares it with a consented reference model.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [GLiClass edge](https://huggingface.co/knowledgator/gliclass-edge-v3.0) and [newer model variants](https://huggingface.co/knowledgator/collections) already ship; runtimes are taken. Exact workload/phone result **Open experiment**. **Platform ownership:** OS/model vendors can ship inference; private task distributions and parity evidence belong to users. **Start:** define a consented local dataset with held-out tool labels and explicit abstention; run on one ordinary phone. **Kill:** if quality fails required error rates or runtime energy is impractical, publish the boundary; if an existing model already meets it, upstream a deployment recipe. **E — Scores:** **4 / 4 / 2 / 4 / 4 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 4 / 2**.

### P08 — Which login owns this subscription?

**Q — Source:** Ben Davis, [V31 06:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O8cl4R5VEOI&t=360s), “I forget which login I used.” His paid Axiom account required support despite browser/email tools and multiple connected accounts.

**I — One sentence:** create a local evidence notebook mapping a purchase to candidate provider, login method, email and organization, distinguishing confirmed ownership from guesses. **Size/E:** Small personal tool or Tiny password-manager recipe. **Beneficiaries:** people with several personal/work identities. **Demo/E:** a receipt yields two candidate logins; an observed signed-in billing page confirms one, with a timestamped evidence link and no account guessing from receipt address alone.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [provider account lookup](https://support.grammarly.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000090171-Which-account-am-I-being-billed-for), [login-method guidance](https://help.tractive.com/hc/en-us/articles/33455131802130-I-paid-for-a-subscription-but-the-app-is-asking-me-to-subscribe-again) and password-manager notes already exist. Cross-provider evidence view **Open hypothesis**, weak demand evidence. **Platform ownership:** each service knows its own account; no service has the complete user's cross-provider map. Password managers are natural incumbents. **Start:** manually resolve ten past subscriptions using existing notes and record which facts were missing. **Kill:** if a standard vault custom field solves it, publish that template; if evidence requires support, show a prepared evidence bundle rather than promise recovery. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 2 / 3 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 2 / 2**.

### P09 — Final Cut handoff tested from the recipient's side

**Q — Source:** Ben Davis, [V31 12:00–16:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O8cl4R5VEOI&t=720s), wants assets prepared and “ready to edit”; the existing context knows too much, so he simulates the recipient in a fresh thread.

**I — One sentence:** encode one editor's actual acceptance task and test a handoff on a clean receiving machine, beyond confirming media paths. **Size/E:** Small skill/fixture. **Beneficiaries:** small video teams. **Demo/E:** a package passes media-online checks but fails to render the required sequence because a plugin/proxy/role differs; a clean recipient run identifies the exact missing prerequisite.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [PostLab](https://docs.hedge.video/postlab/documents/relink-prevention) already checks per-machine locations and media status. **Shipped** broad readiness mechanisms; only task-specific acceptance conditional. **Platform ownership:** Hedge/Apple can own most of this; an independent recipe captures a team's real output contract. **Start:** two machines, one representative project and a ten-action receiving checklist; compare PostLab alone. **Kill:** no extra failure found beyond existing tooling means submit a workflow example, not a product. **E — Scores:** **4 / 3 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 3 / 1**.

### P10 — Same request, different environment: routing experiment

**Q — Sources:** Ben Davis, [V30 08:00–09:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NUTuk5EwxoM&t=480s), asks what “check my email” means when connector/browser/auth differ; Scott Wu, [V10 11:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=B2Iou-8mu9k&t=660s), predicts frontier models handle “about 2% of workloads.” The percentage is his forecast, not established workload composition.

**I — One sentence:** measure whether supplying environment facts changes the model needed for the same useful task more than changing the prompt or model label. **Size/E:** Tiny benchmark using an existing router. **Beneficiaries:** agent integrators deciding where inference savings are real. **Demo/E:** paired requests with a working structured mail connector versus only a browser/missing auth; report useful completion and human intervention, not merely generated text.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [ORBIT](https://github.com/LAMDA-Model-Reuse/ORBIT), [ACRouter](https://github.com/LanceZPF/agent-as-a-router/blob/main/docs/HANDBOOK.md) and [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/academy/experiments) already cover context and execution-grounded routing. **Being built/Shipped**; exact paired corpus conditional. **Platform ownership:** routers should own these signals. Contribute environment-paired cases. **Start:** six task families, two environments each, fixed budget and quality threshold; include a strong-model baseline. **Kill:** if existing router evaluation already covers the pairs or environment features do not help, publish a null result. **E — Scores:** **4 / 5 / 2 / 4 / 3 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 3 / 2**.

### P11 — Retire an obsolete shim without deleting a rare necessary one

**Q — Sources:** Geoff Huntley, [V80 40:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HDdbU10r7Js&t=2400s), says “skills that you use should be deleted after every model release.” Theo, V86 03:00, says “delete a bunch ... not ... using as many ... as I expected.” The question is whether low use proves obsolescence; Geoff explicitly offers a hot take, not an evaluated policy.

**I — One sentence:** extend skill ablation with exposure counts and rare counterexamples so “not used lately” does not mean “safe to remove.” **Size/E:** Tiny upstream contribution. **Beneficiaries:** agent-harness maintainers. **Demo/E:** two instructions are unused on ordinary tasks; one is retired, while a held-out rare failure keeps the other despite identical recent-use counts.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [skill-up](https://github.com/alibaba/skill-up) already runs no-skill ablations and multi-engine/CI evaluation. **Shipped** broad mechanism. **Platform ownership:** this belongs in existing evaluation toolchains; no new skill registry. **Start:** select two real shims with recorded originating failures, then run current and earlier models with/without them. **Kill:** if current fixtures already represent rare exposure adequately, contribute only cases; if the originating failure cannot be reproduced, do not infer safe retirement from non-use. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 2 / 4 / 3 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 4 / 2**.

### P12 — Does cheaper coding save money or expand scope?

**Q — Sources:** Theo, [V85 25:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FHGfSjDGUIw&t=1500s), “the VM that runs on is going to be more expensive than the inference”; Krentsel, V52 03:00, emphasizes expensive frontier tokens; Michelle, V12 40:00–43:00, predicts subsidies will end. These are conditional economic claims with different denominators.

**I — One sentence:** publish a paired fixed-deliverable versus freely expanded-scope agent-cost study, including human attention, execution and cash/subscription allocation. **Size/E:** Tiny blog/dataset. **Beneficiaries:** individual builders deciding whether an ambitious side project is affordable. **Demo/E:** the same useful change shows lower token cost but more attempts, tests and VM minutes; separate the saved cost at fixed scope from the extra work voluntarily purchased.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Langfuse experiments](https://langfuse.com/academy/experiments) and mature cost accounting already exist. **Shipped**, low vacancy. **Platform ownership:** providers know their charges but not all user attention or counterfactual scope. Use existing telemetry. **Start:** run three real tasks under predeclared acceptance versus expanded scope, report denominators and historical prices explicitly. **Kill:** if the report cannot value useful output independently of token volume, do not call it productivity. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 4 / 1**.

### P13 — Cancel, steer, and late-effect conformance fixtures

**Q — Source:** Alex Krentsel, [V52 31:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5lFD-34dhqE&t=1860s), “better notion of ... interruptible work.” Amp already demonstrates queued steering; that is evidence of an implementation, not an unbuilt primitive.

**I — One sentence:** contribute fixtures for cancellation acknowledgement, prompt-after-cancel, pending permission and late side effects across two existing ACP adapters. **Size/E:** Tiny upstream PR. **Beneficiaries:** client/adapter maintainers and users whose stop action currently has ambiguous results. **Demo/E:** cancel a task, immediately send a new prompt, release a delayed tool result and show each adapter's actual events and external effects.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [ACP v2](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol/blob/main/docs/protocol/v2/migration.mdx) explicitly covers lifecycle; [Prime's report](https://github.com/PrimeIntellect-ai/prime-agent/discussions/2305) and [Copilot's report](https://github.com/github/copilot-cli/issues/4561) identify specific 2026 cases. **Being built**, no empty protocol category. **Platform ownership:** adapters/protocol maintainers should own these checks. **Start:** reproduce one report on pinned revisions, then submit a fixture that fails before a targeted fix. **Kill:** if already fixed with equivalent coverage, link the test and move on. A cancellation acknowledgement must not be treated as proof that an earlier external effect was undone. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 4**; **a/b/c: 5 / 4 / 1**.

### P14 — Cases alone versus cases plus reasoning: a controlled research seed

**Q — Source:** Gabe/Harvey, [V34 18:00–20:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TU8kwE7z1qY&t=1080s), “I don't know how to measure this.” He reports that adding long-tail cases, including training, did not reproduce performance on widely discussed cases, and hypothesizes the missing surrounding analysis.

**I — One sentence:** test whether human commentary improves transfer beyond merely adding the same case facts and more tokens. **Size/E:** Ambiguous; Tiny protocol, weeks of expert data/grading. **Beneficiaries:** legal-research evaluators and domain-model builders. **Demo/E:** show matched cases-only, cases-plus-commentary and length-matched controls on held-out reasoning tasks, graded blind by a qualified reviewer.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [commentary generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24534) and [legal-reasoning data](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ciol-research/multilevel-legal-reasoning) are active. **Being built** broadly; narrow causal test unresolved by this search. **Platform ownership:** labs and legal vendors are likely owners; a transparent small protocol is a contribution. **Start:** secure an expert collaborator and eligible case/commentary pairs, explicitly audit training/publication leakage and fact/length confounds. **Kill:** no independent expert or valid matched set means keep it as a protocol note; if benefit disappears with length/context controls, reject the commentary hypothesis. **E — Scores:** **4 / 2 / 2 / 4 / 3 / 5**; **a/b/c: 3 / 3 / 2**.

### P15 — Adversarial fixtures for a private-to-public repository projection

**Q — Source:** Ree, [V11 1:08–1:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bs3xRsDItiI&t=4080s), “the main repo is just like a clean mirror,” describing separate private development history and public source.

**I — One sentence:** contribute tricky renamed-file, metadata and generated-asset fixtures to an existing Copybara configuration. **Size/E:** Tiny PR. **Beneficiaries:** maintainers already publishing an OSS projection. **Demo/E:** an innocuous rename causes private text to enter an export fixture; the existing transformation is repaired and the regression remains reproducible.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Copybara](https://github.com/google/copybara/blob/master/docs/reference.md) already transforms source and metadata. **Shipped**, generic mirror idea killed. **Platform ownership:** existing export tooling should own fixes. **Start:** obtain a sanitized real export policy and write one failing fixture. **Kill:** if configuration docs already cover it and no reproducible leak exists, only improve the example. Do not claim the checks prove arbitrary private information cannot escape. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 1 / 3 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 3 / 1**.

### P16 — TDD versus fewer tests, with an external outcome oracle

**Q — Sources:** Adam, [V55 36:00–38:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aYz9G3__Z4g&t=2160s), asks “whether unit tests provide much value at this point”; Eric, [V61 03:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4wBVuj2an8E&t=180s), endorses “follow the test driven development.” Matt Pocock, V23, advocates disciplined testing; Armin/Ben, V82, differ over expensive tests versus release gates. These are task-dependent positions, not universal opposites.

**I — One sentence:** run the same maintenance tasks under TDD, acceptance-only and unconstrained conditions, with a hidden external acceptance suite that does not reward following a prescribed process. **Size/E:** Tiny benchmark extension. **Beneficiaries:** OSS maintainers choosing testing effort. **Demo/E:** a test-heavy run scores well on process but misses a user-visible behavior; a different run passes the independent outcome while spending less time, or the reverse.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [RigorBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22678) already tests discipline. **Released**, no new broad evaluation category. **Platform ownership:** benchmark authors are natural owners. **Start:** contribute three tasks with independent regressions and report output correctness, latency and cost separately from process adherence. **Kill:** if test generation/acceptance leaks between conditions or tasks merely restate the implementation, the comparison is invalid. **E — Scores:** **3 / 5 / 1 / 3 / 3 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 3 / 1**.

### P17 — Did the work teach you enough to repair it a week later?

**Q — Sources:** Sam Newman, V20 1:40–1:43, warns about cognitive debt; Peter Mattis, [V22 1:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0GzwuYGvKA4&t=5940s), says he “learned more ... year than previous 5 years combined.” Different people and tasks, same interview channel: a hypothesis pair, not a contradiction already settled by anecdotes.

**I — One sentence:** compare delegation with active explanation/teachback on a real small project, then measure unaided repair and transfer after a delay. **Size/E:** Tiny self-study, Big only as a multi-person longitudinal study. **Beneficiaries:** builders learning a new domain while shipping. **Demo/E:** both conditions finish today; one week later a related bug is introduced, and the user explains and fixes it without AI.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Anthropic's January 2026 study](https://www.anthropic.com/research/AI-assistance-coding-skills) already finds that engagement matters in short-term skill formation. Existing explanatory/learning modes rule out a new generic tutor. **Released research/Shipped modes**; delayed own-project transfer is an extension. **Platform ownership:** providers can offer learning modes; independent users can measure the repair skill they actually need. **Start:** predeclare two comparable unfamiliar modules and a delayed repair rubric; avoid reusing the exact training task. **Kill:** if carryover or task mismatch dominates the tiny sample, report personal observations rather than a causal result. **E — Scores:** **4 / 4 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 3 / 1**.

### P18 — A depth-camera friendship mural recipe

**Q — Source:** Sunil, [V12 53:00–55:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gPBw5Cr9VJo&t=3180s), describes his digital depth-camera mural and asks for “things of beauty for friendship” and “software that people use.” He is already building an example.

**I — One sentence:** publish a reproducible recipe for a physical gathering's shared visual artifact, designed with the actual friends who will use it. **Size/E:** Small art weekend; Tiny reproducibility write-up. **Beneficiaries:** a particular group of friends/community, not an assumed market. **Demo/E:** two people enter the space, their motion contributes to a shared image, and the group leaves with a chosen still artifact.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [TDOAK](https://github.com/TouchDesigner/TDOAK) already provides depth-camera integration. **Shipped tooling/Being built example**, not empty. **Platform ownership:** vendors supply sensors/rendering; a group's aesthetic purpose is local. **Start:** borrow a supported camera and interview the group about what they would enjoy; use existing TouchDesigner components. **Kill:** if the group prefers an ordinary photo or sees no value in the installation, keep it as an art experiment. **E — Scores:** **5 / 3 / 1 / 3 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 5 / 2 / 1**.

### P19 — Compare a reference game and its remake under the same input trace

**Q — Source:** Theo, [V01 16:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NBCM_ZoWZo8&t=960s), “record my controller inputs” and “compare how well my game behaves.” The point is matching motion/controls, not only screenshots.

**I — One sentence:** add a small cross-game comparison adapter that feeds a trace to a reference and remake, then shows trajectory/timing divergence with an explicit tolerance. **Size/E:** Small/Tiny upstream prototype. **Beneficiaries:** hobby game remakers and regression testers. **Demo/E:** identical flick-stick input produces visibly different jump timing; align the action landmarks and show where behavior diverges.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Qaping Replay](https://qaping.dev/docs/replay) already supplies controller recording/replay and receipts; [Frame Perfect](https://www.frameperfect.dev/docs) compares recorded states. **Shipped/Being built**, low vacancy. Qaping explicitly does not promise deterministic gameplay. **Platform ownership:** game QA projects should own the adapter. **Start:** one controllable reference, one remake, fixed starting state, measured reset variance. **Kill:** if ordinary replay plus frame comparison answers the question, contribute a recipe; if RNG/camera changes swamp behavior differences, do not assign an automatic correctness score. **E — Scores:** **5 / 4 / 1 / 3 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 2 / 1**.

### P20 — A personal media-editing extension, rather than another malleable editor

**Q — Sources:** Adam, [V55 32:00–33:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aYz9G3__Z4g&t=1920s), wants a “platform of primitives … personal applications” for creative software; Scott, 33:00, describes “editing my wife's podcast.” Sunil, V12 54:00–55:00, wants personally useful and beautiful software; Ben/Modem, V80 1:33, suggests one useful customization can be enough.

**I — One sentence:** implement one creator's repeated editing operation as a reversible extension inside an existing malleable editor. **Size/E:** Ambiguous until the creator supplies a task, then a Small weekend extension. **Beneficiaries:** one actual creator, with potential to share the recipe. **Demo/E:** a creator's own caption/cut/asset convention becomes one undoable action, then survives reopening the project.

**F/I — Prior art/status:** [Powermove](https://github.com/iterative-computer/powermove) already implements a self-extensible video editor with typed, undoable live transactions. **Shipped/released** broad concept. **Platform ownership:** the editor should own the extension API; the user's peculiar operation is the contribution. **Start:** observe a creator repeating the same edit three times, compare existing commands/plugins, and contribute a minimal extension if still useful. **Kill:** existing commands or one prompt solve it reliably, or the operation never recurs. **E — Scores:** **4 / 4 / 1 / 4 / 5 / 5**; **a/b/c: 4 / 2 / 1**.

## Preserved odd and ambiguous directions that are not yet project proposals

**I — Do not discard small human desires:** elk-call practice and GeoCities-scale hobby apps (V09), a Waymo counter (V11), meetup organization and fish feeding (V12), sewing/seam geometry and personal media tools (V55/V62), reading explanations, family apps (V56) and a friendship mural all remain in the source board. Some are already products; some are already the speaker's personal creation; some are too thin to name an unmet user. The aquarium search even returns open monitoring/feeding implementations, such as [AquaNexis](https://github.com/rokayaraka/AquaNexis), which is evidence of work, not verified hardware reliability. A consumer seed becomes actionable when a real person can name the unwanted step and agree on what “better” means. P01 is a way to discover that without assuming the answer is an assistant or a platform.

**I — Keep the abstraction wish separate:** Sunil's V49 04:00–05:00 “no one has built the React yet” is a strong expression of dissatisfaction but a weak specification. Portable agent composition is already crowded. Resolve it by collecting three failed compositions whose requirements cannot be met in existing systems; until then this is an ambiguous research direction, not P21 or permission to invent another framework.

**I — Disagreements worth testing:** (1) continual personal editing versus one-time tailoring → P01; (2) model taste versus human product insight → P03; (3) strong model versus cheap specialized model under different tools → P10; (4) inference savings versus scope/compute expansion → P12, with denominator correction; (5) tests as indispensable scaffold versus costly model-era residue → P16; (6) AI as cognitive debt versus accelerated learning → P17. Only the taste exchange is an explicit direct debate about the same claim. Others require matched tasks to become contradictions.

**I — Killed/rejected broad pitches stay killed:** generic durable hosts, snapshots, Pi/brain-on-DO, code-mode runtimes, JIT agents, GPU-kernel optimization, general auth/payments/MCP, Git storage, TS compiler ports, Wasm ports, generated-app hosts, generic memory/replay and generic skill libraries. New quotes support narrowly scoped experiments or upstream checks, not a reset of prior novelty research. Cloudflare's missing hibernation observability, migration rehearsals and hot-shard measurements remain credible operational contributions in the Connect ledgers, but have platform owners and are not independently promoted here as new S05 products.

## Complete predictions and wishes board

**F — Inclusion rule:** every requested-type row plus roadmap rows; supplemental recall scan adds other rows whose quote contains a forward-looking/modal word (`will`, `won't`, `going to`, `gonna`, `wish`, `want`, `hope`, `predict`, `eventually`, `future`, `next`, `would`, `could`, `might`, `should`, `soon`, `become`, `becomes`, `need`, `needs`). Historical uses are deliberately retained and marked supplemental. Core types include plain Wish and mixed labels, so no narrow spelling filter drops a wish. Connect's `Pr, hypothetical` is retained. A row appearing in several categories is counted once.

**I — Interpretation rule:** E-ledger implication and build cells below retain the extraction's conditional interpretation, not a new vacancy assertion; Connect build cells translate the talk's claim into a required capability and preserve occupied ownership. For an established product/roadmap the implication is “that owner must ship/operate it,” not “start a clone.” Main field-map status supersedes old extraction novelty suggestions. Unranked rows have **no independent field-emptiness finding in this report**; their original ledgers contain their own triage. Macro forecasts can imply no bounded software artifact.
